## 2026-10-08 — Method change: non-destructive current sensing selected for investigation

Overall stage: analog block validation, before complete PLIF integration. The destructive charge-read/capture/inverse/rewrite path remains unaccepted; repeated switch tuning has not removed its millivolt errors. Suspend further dummy-sizing sweeps and prioritize a small resident sense transistor with shared column readout, keeping membrane charge in its capacitor. This is a selected experiment, not an accepted chip architecture.

New scripts/screen_gf180_nondestructive_current_read.py (source cbf0ccec1dba) completed matched 20ns/10ns nominal runs at three states with two reads each. artifacts/analog_asic/results/gf180_nondestructive_current_read_boundary.json audits every recorded hash and exact deck equality except tran. Maximum post-second-read displacement is 3.086uV relative to the already-written pre-read state; step dependence reaches approximately 0.875uV, so this is not a precision/noise qualification. Temporary read coupling is 1.47–8.11mV and initial write-release error remains 16.81–33.41mV. Read currents are monotonic at these three points, but nonlinear; no implemented decoder/calibration. This does not demonstrate accurate state initialization or complete neural updates.

Native NF0.5um/L1um and 7um MIM are schematic; write/select/park TGs and clock inverters use qualified cap-extracted components. Ideal shared 1.65V column clamp and zero-drain parking remain bench assumptions. Zero parking avoids the modeled continuous sense-channel DC path; leakage, noise, mismatch, PVT, finite array loading and standby power remain unqualified. No sense-cell layout or new physical acceptance. All launched runs completed.

Alternatives: (1) non-destructive current read with shared column electronics is the next candidate; (2) local switched-capacitor updates can apply learned beta once per bin, signed weighted accumulation, threshold and subtractive reset in place, but require area/noise evaluation; (3) calibrated destructive read/restore remains a fallback with nonlinear inverse and switch-error burden. Uncontrolled continuous-time leakage is not a substitute for the trained bin equation. A switched-capacitor neuromorphic implementation is documented at https://arxiv.org/abs/1412.3233; it establishes precedent, not feasibility for this population.

Next milestone: implement and characterize the sense cell and a real shared column frontend, reduce initial-write error, then integrate one complete analog PLIF neuron preserving the original float model equations. Evaluate model tolerance, 5ms population service and realistic area before full-chip assembly/layout. No QAT/digital fallback. Original-float accuracy, complete PLIF, full service and full-chip layout remain unproven; goal active/incomplete. No critical user decision is needed for this reversible investigation.

Sessions 78140 (100 uV), 40055 (no-noise control), 1311 (30 uV) and 50978 (zero-amplitude writeback control) are terminal/completed. All use original float record 0, all 25 actors/full frame, linear gain 1 and ideal per-record channel scaling. Both controls exactly reproduce baseline spikes and segmentation (IoU 0.2057260126); the zero-amplitude writeback path matches for all three seeds. Thus the implemented noise wrapper alone does not explain the observed loss. At 100 uV independent initialization/every-bin state noise, mean IoU is 0.1216130778; at 30 uV it is 0.1216079493. No noise tolerance acceptance threshold inferred.

These are ideal algorithmic mappings, not implemented encoder acceptance. They indicate that storage encoding and linear computation precision must be addressed separately. Inspected existing original-float full-frame writeback-every-bin replays. ## 2026-10-08 — Full-inventory architecture capacity evidence

Executed scripts/analyze_gf180_architecture_capacity.py against the original checkpoint hash, 25-actor full-frame inventory, audited native 7x7 MIM geometry and routed eight-cap feedback bank. Report: artifacts/analog_asic/results/gf180_architecture_capacity_boundary.json. One 7x7 plate per 30,976,000 membrane identities extrapolates to 1517.824 mm2 of plates; differential pairs double this. This excludes 10,982,400 adaptation-enabled states and switches/peripherals. Isolated-cell bounding-box replication is 3124.23936 mm2, not a packed-layout minimum. Required all-resident bin service is 6.1952 billion updates/second; events and graph work are additional.

The report separates resident storage from serial feedback replication. Illustratively allocating half of a hypothetical 300 mm2 chip to membrane plates permits 4.84246 um2 per state; allocating one quarter to the current feedback plates permits only an optimistic 3.12659 us serial slot. Neither allocation, geometry nor slot is qualified. The measured 25 us encoding phase alone exceeds this slot before reset/event/restore overhead. These are explicit feasibility constraints, not a die-size requirement or proof that another topology cannot work.

Next: evaluate smaller analog storage and original-float error tolerance together, then a faster complete neuron service topology. Do not replicate the current 7um resident capacitor/25us service design as a suitable chip. Preserve the full inventory and analog semantics; no quantized fallback, assumed sparse-state omission or resolution reduction. Full-chip/layout/service/accuracy completion remains unproven.

## 2026-10-08 — Status correction and article scope

The user requests a suitable ASIC implementation for an article; manufacturing readiness and perfect device behavior are not the target. The original full-float SNN remains the reference, with no QAT requirement or digital fallback. Research claims still require faithful neuron semantics, defensible accuracy and service estimates, and real connectivity for any layout described as implemented.

Current result: circuit experiments and reusable primitive layouts, not an end-to-end ASIC. The completed integrity audit examined 28 imported views: 13 passed full-cell DRC and exported-GDS connectivity checks; 15 did not. A separately corrected phase inverter passes those checks and brings the import inventory to 29. This is 14 passing views across the original audit and the separate correction, not a fresh 29-view audit. Earlier import counts and narrow DRC/LVS flags must not be described as full physical qualification. Legacy simulations remain intended-circuit experiments where their layout failed export checks.

Two routed MIM banks pass the stronger audit. Small nominal writeback experiments show approximately 0.2–1.4% preservation loss for an intermediate candidate, with incomplete switching cases retained. They do not establish a complete PLIF, validation-set accuracy, or 5 ms service. An optimistic 25 us / eight-feedback-MIM serial-service scenario needs about 599.7 mm2 of feedback plates alone for the resident population; this is a conditional architecture warning, not a full-chip area estimate.

All launched simulations and the integrity audit are terminal; no simulator is running. First-order Gear variants aborted, and the native reset-resistor variant did not provide a selected remedy. Historical launch entries below are superseded by this status.

Next priority: resolve architecture-level service and density, establish a tolerable error envelope on the original float model, and implement a representative weighted-event PLIF macro including threshold and subtractive reset. Use that macro for simulation and layout evidence with explicit chip-level extrapolation. Repair only the physical views required by the selected macro. Repeated local precision refinements are subordinate to these article objectives. Complete PLIF, full-model accuracy, 5 ms service, full-chip assembly and article acceptance remain open.

Older status paragraphs below are chronological and are superseded wherever they conflict with this gate.

2026-10-02 update: A complementary physical-trim schematic now gives correct ±q0.1 decisions on the physical MIM comparator at 1000× exploratory state scale for one deterministic paired ±1σ mismatch orientation and its mirror (with different trim codes). A VCM-referenced single DAC did not. One extracted xhigh-poly resistor PCell passes DRC/LVS, but the complete R-2R network remains ideal in these benches. Next physical work: build and extract a small matched ladder section, characterize code transfer and mismatch/noise, then expand to the full trim bank. Do not treat this local comparator result as system acceptance; retain the independent event-update, state-retention, noise and 5ms service gates above.

2026-10-01 state-dependent charge continuation: a TT/27°C physical100µm² MOS gate bank was calibrated at11 levels from0.15–1.65V, then driven by ideal state-aware current pulses computed from the original full-float weights and an eight-range nonlinear membrane map. The high-cancellation322-event fan-in (17.71µs at55ns slots) matched the ideal encoded state within19µV at sampled prefixes; a370-event large-swing fan-in (20.35µs) ended2.87mV from target while the state crossed the measured MOS C(V) curve. The inferred bank capacitance varies from0.276pF to0.810pF across the calibration span. Continue by replacing the ideal charge command with a realizable analog event driver and physical state feedback, then replay signed fan-in and measure closed-loop error before layout. Evidence: `scripts/screen_sky130_mos_state_aware_charge.py` and `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_mos_state_aware_ideal_charge_boundary.json`.

A bounded SKY130 physical OTA gate-precharge experiment (`sky130_ms_weight_gate_prefetch_ss0_n0p48.json`) changes a loaded NMOS gate from a 0.45V precharge to a 0.48V command: output error is -4.57mV at 0.2us, +9.69mV at 0.5us, -1.45mV at 2us and -1.25mV at 3us, compared with the cold branch's -414mV at 2us and about 7-9us to settle. 2026-09-29 refinement: for two100µm MIM caps, reducing the20.31µV independent differential reset proxy to illustrative3µV/1µV would need reset-noise correlation `rho≥0.9782/0.9976`; these are design probes, not acceptance limits. A tighter physical injector two-point PVT check confirms that scalar gain trim is insufficient: at gate fractions0.4/0.6 the normalized charge ratio varies0.0364 (SS/0°C),0.0424 (TT/27°C), and0.0588 (FF/85°C). The low TT/SS points are stable to0.05ns timestep under tightened tolerances, while earlier loose multi-case curves shifted materially. Next qualify a reproducible full signed-weight transfer table and physical nonlinear calibration/reference mechanism, including low weights. Do not use the loose small-signal curve as a weight specification.

Tight per-case PVT curves at gate fractions0.1–0.95 are monotonic but leave only0.0986–1165.34µV common single-event charge across the selected corners. Under the illustrative0.9V per-stage scaling from three float patches, fixed10.5ns pulses cover66.74% of143,728 PLIF static weights (plus96 final head weights outside this count). A fixed per-stage pulse width could cover the largest sampled weight with at most444.1ns at the slow corner; SS/0°C fraction0.95 is near-linear through450ns at zero state, and the sampled per-stage p99 serialized pulse contribution is≤15.74µs. These are promising range/service bounds, not an implementation: the0.1-fraction signal is below noise qualification, a450ns event varies5.35% over−0.9…+0.9V prepared state, low-end width scaling is unmeasured, and physical gate DAC/calibration and weighted fan-in remain open. Keep layout gated and next test a state-aware calibrated signed event source and its actual float-model impact.

The signed repeated-event check now bounds one concrete accumulation error: ten0.95-fraction450ns events at SS/0°C are0.822% low against ten isolated events, with±0.2657mV residual after alternating signs. At a40ns final-fusion-like width, same-sign error is0.0731% but alternating residual remains±2.135µV, about11% of the sampled minimum final-fusion threshold under the illustrative scaling. It is a matched simulator-control comparison, not an on-chip correction. Next use actual float weight signs/magnitudes and bin ordering on a physically initialized cell, then replay measured nonlinearity through the float model before claiming SNN fidelity.

2026-09-29 physical follow-up: the simple per-state20µm MIM/OTA held-output baseline sampler does not cancel a controlled−20/0/+20µV initial differential into a stable hardware decision. Its no-event output-minus-held value spans0.901mV, larger than the≈0.74–0.77mV output change from one≈4.12µV event; all tested comparator outputs remain high at the ideal15.5mV offset. The event-minus-no-event transfer only looks stable after simulator subtraction, which is not hardware. Do not use this OTA/output-MIM cell as the correlated reference. The next bounded analog path is a physically shared low-noise initialization/reference or a state-storage element with materially greater capacitance density; require actual reference cancellation and measured noise before selecting either. The two-cap geometry/correlation sweep in the architecture report shows why simply shrinking SKY130 MIM demands correlation above0.9997 at10µm for the illustrative3µV probe.

The near-threshold gate is decisive for this OTA cell: at gate fraction0.601, a≈4.18–4.20µV event produces final second-OTA outputs1.756/1.609/0.230V for−20/0/+20µV initial offsets. The+20µV case alone crosses the nominal0.9V logic boundary despite nearly identical event charge. This is deterministic TT, not noise yield, but it rules out claiming the held-output baseline cancels per-state initialization uncertainty. Preserve this as a failed comparator architecture and do not spend further layout effort on it.

## Immediate bounded plan — weight delivery, 2026-09-24

Decision: preserve one logical presynaptic event -> one signed weighted contribution using ordinary full-float trained parameters; QAT/quantized versions do not define analog precision. The direct differential MOS injector with fixed10.5ns pulses and gate amplitude into a matched20um MIM pair remains the best current burst schematic branch, but not accepted. The corrected maximum-fan-in site gives0.81–0.96% error and8.4uV order span at gain0.965; gain0.986 improves a selected near-threshold event to<0.18% error but worsens the large-sum site's error to1.34–1.61% and order span to23.5uV. The10um MOS state pair shows6.3–6.6% error. An ideal1nS common-mode servo holds common mode but increases order spread to52.2uV. Longer or full-bin event spacing is not a robust fix: the5ms-spread MOS sequence has194.3uV order span. There is no global trim or smaller-cap MOS pass. The passive switched-capacitor fallback is now under a matched full-float weighted-event screen because packet charge may add more linearly; a10um/1um membrane/packet pair requires about0.55mV state change per50mV top-plate step and a measured ratio for the weight map. After this comparison, continue with whichever circuit has better multi-site error and retention, then add physical threshold/readout/reset and local spike replay. SKY130 remains active; ASAP7 storage and digital/XL artifacts are preserved history. No layout, population scaling, or network-accuracy claim is qualified. This continuation supersedes the earlier passive-SC-lead statement below.

1. **Freeze the full-float reference and an error contract.** The current primary is `artifacts/model_checkpoints/dsec_6class/EventSpikeSeg140k_seed17_best.pt` (epoch 58, best of 80, `is_best=true`); retain `EventSpikeSeg140k_seed17_epoch080.pt` as the final-epoch comparison (`is_best=false`). Record the SHA-256 hashes and both configurations. Neither file has ASIC-QAT provenance metadata; QAT and quantized exports are unnecessary for this analog path and must not define its weight precision. The full-float best checkpoint has been exported to `artifacts/analog_asic/model_reference/`. Use the active 25-actor DAG and folded ChannelAffine gains. Do not guess missing metadata. Use small real parameter subsets/short input traces from ordinary PLIF, stem inhibition, adaptation and readout; no full hardware-model replay. Report weight distributions per layer/channel, small-weight mass, signed sum/state ranges and near-threshold margins. Compare the pulse-tick/circuit transfer mapping with those same float traces; do not substitute synthetic low-bit model weights. Select scaling and error budget from observed state/spike/readout changes; task-level accuracy tolerance remains unset until supported by a bounded software validation comparison and an explicit acceptance criterion. Do not silently prune, clip or fine-tune.
2. **Compare pulse duration against fast fixed-width gate-amplitude events.** Earlier long logical pulse-duration service estimates still exceed 5ms for many fan-in locations. The newer transistor-level fixed-bias width mapping is separately tested and also fails the 370-event weighted screen (6.7x target error at full gate bias; 33x at a reduced bias). Keep it as a documented comparison unless a regulated source materially changes transfer.
3. **Decide from accuracy AND throughput.** Translate measured transfer/timing into the real float traces. Preserve all contributions before one threshold and one beta step per bin; no unfinished packets or error carried into a future bin/event. A first scale screen (per layer, largest effective float weight mapped to the maximum tested pulse) predicts median single-event positive durations of 162–265us in sampled layers. Replaying active inputs through the three sampled convolutions predicts serialized fan-in service medians of 0.19ms for stem, 6.58ms for enc1.standard and 15.94ms for final_fuse.standard, before setup/settling; 61.6% and 77.6% of active enc1/final-fuse destinations exceed 5ms. This is an optimistic one-lane-per-output screen, not a complete schedule, and no accuracy criterion has been set. It makes serial pulse delivery unsuitable at these scales without substantial within-neuron parallelism. Assess the switched-capacitor fallback now against the same float trace; do not represent pulse duration as qualified. Include existing restore/ADC costs and joins in any eventual 5ms claim.
4. **Passive switched-capacitor membrane comparison (early schematic evidence only).** The MOS virtual-summing-node OTA integrator is demoted: physical-threshold mixed `pn`/`np` runs differ by 0.73µV, individual event windows move the stored state by about 6mV nearly independent of a 50mV-to-53.5mV packet adjustment, and keeping both OTAs continuously active drifts by 2.62mV. A new passive SKY130 membrane screen uses a 100µm MIM state capacitor, 1µm MIM packet capacitor and physical TGs, with no OTA during accumulation. At ±50mV top-plate steps it delivers ±5.6µV; `pn` and `np` both return to the initial state within reported precision, and the first-event state shifts by only 0.0–0.1µV over the following ~4ms. This is nominal TT schematic evidence with ideal state initialization: threshold-DAC loading, comparator/read kick, PVT, mismatch/noise, extraction and float-weight mapping are not yet tested. A 1µm packet is linear at5.6/16.8/33.6µV per ±50/±150/±300mV; 3µm reaches≈268µV but has≈1µV mixed-order residual; 10µm reaches≈2.9mV but shows≈28µV same-sign packet variation and2.4µV hold movement. Passive threshold init now uses the physical R2R24 DAC through TGs with no `.ic`: code3060 produces−11.0µV signal-minus-reference through4.9ms versus−10.914µV target. `pn`/`np` at1µm, ±0.3V finish at−10.9/−10.8µV, within0.014/0.114µV of target before comparator kick. The state-range test exposes a new blocker: with packet baseline fixed at0.9V, a1µm ±150mV packet has correct sign at0.9V but gives +62/+30µV for `p`/`n` from0.75V and −32/−62µV from1.05V. A10µm packet is worse. Bootstrapping the packet baseline to the initial state restores the first-event sign but retains 7–9% gain variation and≈119µV hold drift over4ms; increasing the explicit shunt from1e15Ω to1e18Ω does not remove the drift. Passive-cap float replay, with ideal continuous amplitude control and linearized transfer, gives at0.30V candidate headroom: 10µm caps produce0.081%/0.308%/1.283% spike mismatch in stem/enc1/final_fuse; 5.95% of final_fuse weights fall below the provisional0.18µV per-event floor. These are optimistic software sensitivities, not physical results. Later multi-event tests showed that this passive packet topology compresses at high fan-in; retain it as a comparison while exploring state-independent charge-delivery and differential-integration circuits. Do not accept the passive cell or start layout yet. The previous float-map screen double-applied ChannelAffine gain and was wrong; `scripts/analyze_sky130_pulse_float_mapping.py` now treats exported `weight__*` arrays as already-folded effective coefficients. The corrected full-graph sensitivity replay remaps only `final_fuse.standard` through measured 1/3/10µm cap curves and drops weights below the smallest measured packet: at 0.15/0.30/0.60V candidate headroom, 54.6/37.4/26.1% of its coefficients fall below that floor, final-stage spike mismatches are 5.28/3.77/2.55%, and segmentation argmax agreement on three 64×64 patches is 91.87/93.55/94.17%. This is a sensitivity study, not task accuracy or a qualified analog mapping. Matched same-phase no-event subtraction removes a ~7.38µV startup/feedthrough offset; both top-plate pulse signs are monotonic from 5–300mV. New physical-init/hold/packet sequencing results supersede the prior init-failure summary: 2.5µs TG initialization plus a 0.5µs guard reaches 0.75/0.90/1.05V within 109µV; OTA-disconnected 5ms idle retention is within 0.247mV; and a 1µm ±0.3V packet after the hold produces −41.0…−41.3µV / +41.1…+41.2µV when the packet bottom and OTA disconnect before the packet top plate returns to VCM. A two-packet test including packet-cap reset between events gives ±82µV for same-sign pairs; mixed-sign pairs stay within 0.43µV of zero. Maximum error against two single-packet increments is 0.62µV and `pn`/`np` order difference is 0.075µV. A 5ms-bin timing test places events at 0.5ms and 4.5ms and samples after the second event. The first packet signal changes by ≤0.56µV over the intervening ~4ms isolated hold, but after matched reconnect-baseline subtraction the two-event same-sign totals are only −65.3/+65.3µV versus −82.3/+82.3µV expected; mixed-sign residuals are ≤2.31µV. Error reaches 17.03µV and `pn`/`np` order difference 4.20µV. The no-event OTA reconnects shift the stored output by 15.67mV over two windows, so simulator-control subtraction cannot be treated as hardware correction. A 0.5µs post-packet settle tail lowers hold drift to 0.56µV but increases sum error to 17.03µV. OTA bias sleep during hold lowers drift to 0.24µV but retains 14.64mV common reconnect drift; a 0.5µs soft-start lowers drift to 0.10µV but retains 14.54mV shift and 16.90µV accumulation error; precharging OTA output from the membrane is worse (711.7mV common drift, or 890.36mV with bias sleep). These nominal TT experiments do not qualify PVT, mismatch, noise or extraction. The full-float three-patch replay anchored to physically measured ±41.16µV 1µm packets gives 5.325/3.947/2.605% final_fuse spike mismatch and 91.805/93.319/94.092% output pixel argmax agreement at 0.15/0.30/0.60V headroom; this is sensitivity, not task accuracy. The same-phase reference remains a simulator control rather than implemented offset cancellation. The prior active implementation choice was passive switched-capacitor membrane accumulation; the OTA virtual-summing-node path remains a comparison/failure record. Qualify passive transfer and threshold initialization before repeating float-weight sensitivity screens. Do not prune or retrain without a separately agreed accuracy decision. The provisional 2.5µs packet cycle remains an estimate, not throughput qualification.
5. **Qualify only the selected candidate.** Run a small relevant corner/temperature set, model-supported mismatch/noise sensitivity and local layout/extraction for one representative synapse-plus-PLIF module. Missing mismatch data means unqualified variation, not zero mismatch. Integrate all supplies/clock/reference energy and report idle power, energy per logical contribution, settling/throughput and actual area. Include helper circuitry. Then bind variants to the 140k inventory. A nominal schematic pass is not extracted PPA or full-chip layout.
6. **Update the existing status documents.** Record checkpoint hash, chosen precision/scaling, measured-vs-assumed clocks, transfer errors, spike agreement and coverage, service time, and remaining restore/writeback/parallelism blockers in the existing YAML/RESULTS/PROGRESS. Do not describe full-float reference as exact analog float precision. Do not promote the multiplexed state architecture or full ORFS readiness while its ADC/restore accuracy and population throughput remain unqualified. Stop on a measured architectural tradeoff or unavailable physical model; routine bounded tests need no new permission.

Research basis: local `src/eventspikeseg/model_components.py` (`SpikingStage.forward`, `ParametricLIF._candidate_membrane`, `HardwareLIF.forward`); [SpikingJelly neuron model](https://spikingjelly.readthedocs.io/zh-cn/latest/tutorials/en/neuron.html); [pulse-controlled analog synapses](https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/neuroscience/articles/10.3389/fnins.2014.00051/full); [switched-capacitor synapses](https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/neuroscience/articles/10.3389/fnins.2015.00010/full). These demonstrate implementation options, not this project's precision or performance.

## Stage 1 — reproduce and freeze the working baseline

1. Read `docs/project_spec.yaml`, `docs/AGENTS.md`, this file, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` and recent progress/results. Inspect running containers and git changes before editing. Do not overwrite another agent's live changes.
2. Run, in order, from the project root:

   ```powershell
   python scripts/prepare_analog_asic.py
   python scripts/prepare_asap7_analog.py
   python scripts/build_asap7_analog_modules.py
   python scripts/test_asap7_analog.py
   python scripts/check_asap7_analog_ready.py
   ```

   Inspect the JSON and logs, not only exit codes. Readiness is expected to fail initially. ngspice can exit zero after an aborted analysis. Require the intended end time, finite traces and actual assertion results. Do not overwrite newer provenance fields when regenerating manifests; fix generators to preserve or explicitly rebuild those fields first.
3. Record source/PDK/tool/image hashes, command lines and baseline results in machine-readable manifests. Preserve the working baseline before each topology change. Treat 80us as an accelerated functional bench, not the physical 5ms model bin or measured model latency.

Exit: reproducible baseline, correct status reporting, known behavioral placeholders and pending physical work itemized in `design.yaml`.

## Stage 3 — complete the representative PLIF and storage circuitry

**Current status (capacitor gate):** no discrete capacitor SPICE model/PCell is present in the open ASAP7 snapshot (`asap7/capacitor_survey.json`); VDD=0.7V does not establish a capacitor rating or density. MOS Cgg is the user-selected analog experiment and remains unqualified after the retention/leakage diagnostics above. Continue bounded analog architecture exploration; do not silently substitute ideal capacitance or claim a physical view. Digital stays paused unless the user explicitly asks to switch.

1. Qualify an implementable ASAP7 capacitor structure, its model, usable voltage range and layout area. Do not map an ideal capacitor to fabricated geometry without evidence. Include sampling/snapshot/compensation capacitors and buffers, not just one membrane capacitor.
2. Test retention over actual 5ms intervals for multiple signed states and input conditions, reset/feedthrough, charge injection, thermal noise assumptions and relevant temperature/corners. Compare lower-leakage switching/storage designs and capacitance choices using measured sensitivity. Estimate full-population capacitor area before committing to a large value.
3. Replace the logistic comparator, behavioral sense buffer, reset/beta driver and current injector with transistor circuits or fully characterized physical helpers. Keep the behavioral version as a reference. Start with isolated circuits, then integrate one channel; do not debug millions of neurons at once.
4. Implement signed synaptic injection with an explicit weight-delivery circuit (current/charge/pulse-duration coding). Account for weight storage, bias/DAC/control and loading. An ideal B-source is not a physical synapse and its apparent supply power is not valid PPA.
5. Run the representative ordinary PLIF through signed accumulation, threshold/reset, learned-beta update, repeated bins, startup and idle tests. Check nonoverlap, decision capture and settling before commit. Avoid tuning numerical tolerances merely to hide a failure.

Exit: stable representative transistor-level PLIF and physical capacitor candidate, with declared operating limits. If acceptable retention forces infeasible area or a semantic change, present the measured tradeoff as an architecture decision before scaling.

## Stage 4 — finish every module family and bind all actors

| Module family | Required implementation and tests |
| --- | --- |
| Standard/grouped/depthwise/pointwise synapses | Signed weights, tap connectivity, stride/padding/boundaries, multiplicity, pulse loading and delivery throughput; reuse physical cells with verified parameters. |
| Ordinary PLIF | Dedicated local membrane state, programmable learned beta/threshold, sampled comparison/subtractive reset, capture/commit and reset handling. |
| Peer inhibition | Retinotopic cross-channel RMS-derived energy, any-spike trigger, exclude spiking channels, preserve simultaneous spikes and the model's operation order. |
| Adaptation | Per-neuron last-spike age/amplitude, triangular rise/fall and expiry, shared channel penalty, energy-based refresh and threshold adjustment. If digital helpers are chosen, include their state/clock/conversion costs. |
| Energy/reduction | Square/mean/root and channel sums with correct normalization, clipping, signed inputs and 32/64/128-channel cases where the export requires them. |
| Readout | 16-to-6 signed current delivery, ten-bin accumulation, six-class first-index-tie argmax, reset and output coding; no invented output PLIF. |
| Routing and control | Coordinate selection, address delivery, forks, residual OR versus multiplicity-preserving sums, upsample/concat, matching completion tags and backpressure. Include actual digital glue and analog boundaries. |
| Infrastructure | Phase/clock generation, biases/references, supplies, reset distribution, conversion only where needed, output buffering and loading. |

Derive exact variants and multiplicities from the 140k graph. Bind every actor to module implementations and constants in `design.yaml`; report uncovered roles explicitly. Physical memory/compute cannot be relabeled from another layer or PDK without equivalence. Compare small groups against software references before building large groups.

Exit: all module families have implemented netlists, bounded functional tests and a coverage table; no missing function is assigned zero cost.

## Stage 5 — reusable macro layout and extracted characterization

1. Resolve a usable ASAP7 analog layout, DRC, LVS and extraction workflow. The public PDK snapshot contains references to external Calibre decks, not the decks themselves. Inspect available open-source rules and document their actual coverage. Do not claim signoff from incomplete rules or assume commercial licenses. Ask only if an unavailable tool/license or materially weaker validation requires a decision.
2. Lay out one qualified cell/small macro per family, with actual transistors/capacitors, supplies, pins, well/tap requirements, isolation, matching and routing. Reuse accepted cells; avoid separate full optimization for every boundary unless physically necessary.
3. Run connectivity/LVS, available geometric checks, extraction and post-layout SPICE. Preserve all failures and fix the cell before replicating it. Compare schematic/extracted state trajectories, energy and settling; record any checks unavailable in this predictive PDK.
4. Produce consistent GDS, LEF, SPICE/CDL and appropriate Liberty/digital interface views. LEF units, DBU/scaling, layer names, pin access and power pins must match the installed ASAP7 platform. Pay particular attention to ASAP7's scaled library conventions. Timing models must describe digital interfaces honestly; analog nets require custom routing/extraction checks, not invented digital timing arcs.
5. Build `asap7/macro_views.json`, `physical_validation.json` and capacitor qualification evidence with hashes, topology/version, dimensions, corner/loading conditions and measured coverage. Preserve them at accepted milestones.

Exit: physically implemented reusable macros with consistent views and explicit validation status. No empty boxes, copied unrelated GDS, or behavioral sources count as completed analog macros.

## Stage 6 — per-module energy, power, area and latency

For each accepted physical family/variant, run bounded post-extraction activity tests using Stage 2 stimuli. Record supply energy by integrating all relevant V*I sources, idle/static power, incremental active energy with clearly defined baseline, settling/decision latency, initiation interval and area. Include bias, clock/phase, weight delivery, buffers, converters and control, or explicitly mark missing coverage. Avoid double counting shared circuitry and leakage when aggregating.

Cover relevant corners/temperatures and mismatch/noise sensitivity if supported; otherwise provide a labelled uncertainty range. Keep stimulus duration, event count, bin count, capacitance/load and state distribution alongside every value. Distinguish ideal behavioral estimates, schematic transistor results, extracted results and physical measurement. Never treat a training bit width as measured analog precision.

Exit: machine-readable per-module results with units, evidence and assumptions, summarized in existing RESULTS. Missing extracted measurements remain null/unknown.

## Stage 7 — full-model assembly and ORFS preparation

1. Generate a hierarchical 25-actor assembly with all 54 graph edges and all local membrane/adaptation identities conserved. Check coordinate coverage, channel counts, readout mapping, duplicate/aliased state, constant bindings and graph transforms. Reuse hierarchy; do not flatten 31 million neurons for a first attempt.
2. Calculate area and interconnect feasibility from accepted macro dimensions before routing. Produce a full-model architectural floorplan and inspect density, pin count, placement bounds, routing tracks, voltage/bias domains and estimated power distribution. If it cannot fit the selected die/host constraints, stop for a concrete partition/capacity decision rather than silently shrinking the model.
3. Write the actual top/wrappers, SDC, macro placement and power-grid scripts, required view lists and ORFS config under `artifacts/analog_asic/asap7/orfs/`. Use the current digital ORFS platform collateral only where compatible. Configure additional LEF/GDS/Liberty macro views; protect analog instances/nets and record what ORFS routes versus what stays inside custom macros.
4. Upgrade `check_asap7_analog_ready.py` from its current always-not-ready scaffold into a real gate: validate current source/view hashes, matching cells/pins/units, physical qualification, no unresolved black boxes, conservative timing interfaces and full graph/state conservation. File existence alone must not produce a pass. Report scoped limitations separately from blockers.
5. Run a small integration smoke containing real representative macros plus digital glue. Verify loading, synthesis preservation, placement, routing, GDS merge and extracted interface behavior before full-model ORFS.
6. Prepare a reproducible single launcher with preflight, bounded RAM/CPU, logs, durable stage checkpoints and explicit resume behavior. Set NUM_CORES consistently with Docker CPU limits. Schedule by host RAM with headroom; stop an OOM family after failure and resize/repartition instead of sweeping a queue of identical failures. Do not rewrite unchanged inputs on resume. Inspect partial outputs before trusting make timestamps.

Exit: ready=true based on evidence, successful representative integration, and a concrete full-layout launcher. Record the exact Grok/terminal command in this file; do not invent it before validation.

## Stage 8 — run and verify the full physical layout

Once Stage 7 passes, the user's authorization includes executing the analog full-model ORFS flow. Run staged floorplan/placement, clock and power distribution where applicable, routing, extraction and final reports. Save accepted checkpoints; stop on correctness or resource failures rather than multiplying retries. Inspect analog interfaces and routing effects beyond digital STA. Verify GDS integrity, macro count/identity, missing cells, connectivity and available DRC/LVS checks. Do not call the chip complete while unrouted connections or unimplemented circuitry remain.

Full-chip transistor/event simulation is not required. Validate extracted module/interface samples and inventory conservation; keep the intended per-module estimation methodology.

Exit: full routed layout and reports, or a precise feasibility limitation with the completed module layouts/floorplan retained. A limitation is not permission to silently switch technology, reduce scope or restart digital work.

## 2026-09-24 analog follow-up — differential reference

The full-float checkpoint remains the only analog precision reference; quantized/QAT exports are not required. A late-switched VCM-referenced OTA difference readout isolates both membrane nodes during accumulation and cancels the nominal ~15.4mV reconnect shift. All four 5ms-bin event orders work in TT with ≤0.54µV readout relation error, ±63.5µV same-sign packet sums, and <1.9µV mixed residual. Full-float three-patch sensitivity gives 1.78–4.41% final-stage spike mismatch and 92.77–94.39% pixel argmax agreement. A corner screen shows the fixed 1.0 gain under-gains SS by 17.37µV; gain 1.35 corrects SS to 1.07µV but over-gains TT by 21.49µV. First-stage resizing did not cover all corners. Also, the present 1GΩ resistors are ideal components with no physical geometry. The active blocker is now readout calibration plus physically realizable low-loading input resistors. Explore a programmable gain/trim and a switched-capacitor alternative, then return to full corner/mismatch and layout qualification. Evidence is in `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_sc_differential_reference_sampled_*` and `artifacts/analog_asic/model_reference/float_patch_trace_sampled_diffreadout.json`.

## 2026-09-25 analog follow-up — passive sampling is noise-limited; pursue active differential sensing

The SKY130 passive MIM sampler (`scripts/test_sky130_sc_cap_differential_sample.py`) changes the membrane differential by 2.5–3.9µV over its sample window at 10µm, but raw sampled output has about −0.466mV nominal offset. A VCM-only dummy does not remove it in the same run. Offline matched event/no-event subtraction reproduces all four 10µm event-order state changes within 0.05–0.09µV, but that is a simulator control, not a circuit. Estimated kT/C is 142.6µV RMS at 203.8fF; at 1.811pF (30µm) it is 47.8µV RMS, comparable to the ~55µV 5ms two-packet response, while sampling disturbs the differential by 8.1µV. A 1µm cap has ~1.32mV kT/C and ~27mV feedthrough. Do not promote passive voltage sampling as the readout.

A direct OTA differential-input sensitivity screen now shows ±64µV produces roughly ∓0.31V output swing (about 5,000V/V nominal TT); ±250µV approaches the output rails. Continue with an active low-loading differential readout whose closed-loop or integrating gain is set for the real float-model state range, including offset cancellation and 5ms timing. First measure its transfer across signed state steps, then use supported noise/PVT models and full-float sensitivity. Do not infer task accuracy or circuit noise from the present noiseless nominal screens. Evidence: `results/sky130_sc_cap_differential_sample_*_10p0um_bp.json`, `results/sky130_sc_cap_differential_sample_*_30p0um_bp.json`, and `results/sky130_sc_ota_diff_sensitivity.json`.

## Stage 9 — model-level estimates and final handoff

Aggregate exact physical-instance counts with representative or model-derived activity. Report idle/static and active power separately; include routing, clocks, bias, memory/weight delivery and converters without duplication. Provide low/nominal/high activity cases where sparsity is uncertain. Explain uncovered mechanisms.

Compute latency along the DAG with concurrent branches, dependent joins, contention, event delivery and analog settling. Report throughput/5ms-bin completion budget and prediction timing separately from the 50ms observation window. Do not add every layer's isolated delay indiscriminately or present a synthetic smoke duration as inference latency.

Update existing RESULTS with per-module tables, whole-model estimates, layout images/paths, measured-versus-scaled labels, operating conditions and limitations. Update PROGRESS and this file with exact commands, completed gates and outstanding issues. Deliver reusable sources, manifests, physical views and results; no extra Markdown reports. If analog is infeasible, preserve it and ask whether to resume the preserved digital checkpoints.

## Work discipline and stop conditions

Prefer one meaningful representative test before expansion. Preserve last accepted results and track changes by hashes. After two failures with the same apparent cause, change to an evidence-based isolation test or summarize the blocker; do not repeat identical retries or raise limits without a reason. Stop only for an actual external dependency, infeasible area/resource bound, unresolved acceptance criterion affecting claims, or a material architecture/technology decision. Continue analog exploration and debugging within the selected approach; preserve digital/XL checkpoints and switch back only on explicit user instruction. Routine edits, tests, analog ORFS launches after readiness, and fixes within this plan are already authorized.

This plan supersedes historical test-only ownership and blanket no-ORFS instructions. It does not authorize deleting existing artifacts, restarting digital/XL work, purchasing software, bypassing licenses, or claiming a result that has not been obtained.

### Late comparator follow-up — 2026-09-25

At 10ns, the 0.5µm OTA input comparator with 0.8882V precharge disturbed the physical membrane by 0.16–0.19µV. The same-sign `pp`/`nn` cases and mixed `pn` case reported the differential sign correctly, but mixed `np` at −1.953µV was reported positive with +6.162mV paired output difference. This fails near-zero state-sign readout and the 100ns result is timestep-sensitive. Next prototype explicit threshold referencing plus auto-zero/baseline sampling with a switched OTA input and isolated membrane. First demonstrate sign and learned-threshold margin across all four event orders at fine resolution; retain the 0.18µV provisional disturbance/sensitivity budget. If an auto-zeroed low-kick OTA still fails, test a preamplified dynamic latch or buffered closed-loop readout. Do not proceed to layout or claim readout acceptance before this gate. Evidence: `results/sky130_sc_ota_plif_comparator_{pp,nn,pn,np}_w0.5_t1.5us_p0p8882_s10n.json`.

### Current readout candidate — ABBA chopped buffered closed loop

Direct late-switched OTA comparison fails mixed near-zero sign. Retain the switched-capacitor membrane and use two unity-OTA buffers followed by an xhigh-poly closed-loop OTA that reads normal/swapped buffer inputs in A–B–B–A phases. This chopper cancels static output offset and linear drift. The 1MΩ/approximately9.1MΩ geometry estimate was too low: the circuit delivered only ~9.1× gain. Increasing feedback-resistor length from3078µm to3445µm gives effective gain ~10.16× in the physical `pn` transient. With 10ns steps, the 5ms physical-cell `pn`/`np` readouts now show correct polarity, 0.009/−0.005µV input-equivalent total error, and −0.023/+0.020µV read disturbance. At TT amp-only screens with 1.411µV differential and 0.8884V common mode, process/resistor/temperature cases span up to0.068µV input-equivalent gain error. All are nominal SKY130 schematic screens at50ns; QAT/quantized exports are not used.

### State range/noise tradeoff — 2026-09-30

The full-float trace shows severe range pressure: final-fusion max candidate13,801 model units maps to65.21µV/unit at±0.9V, leaving7.14% of sampled threshold margins below the independent10µm-pair192µV reset-noise proxy. A fixed10× voltage gain would overflow18.06% of its sampled candidate values. Ideal hard-saturation replay on two complete validation frames across all25 actors finds32–64× gain lowers Gaussian reset-noise proxy spike mismatch to about4.6–4.9%, but changes roughly20–22% of pixel classes;100× gain collapses task behavior. The per-frame maximum used for each actor is optimistic calibration. Next screen a specific physically realizable nonlinear/split-range analog state and low-noise differential readout, with fixed calibration and measured switching/offset errors. Do not merely increase gain or layout the current independent-cap pair. Keep analog full float, no QAT/digital fallback.

An ideal invertible `asinh` voltage encoding now bounds that research direction: at `R=10000` it has about1010× near-zero gain and lowers the same independent10µm-pair reset-noise proxy to2.31/2.41% final-fusion spike mismatch on two complete frames, but still changes12–13% of pixel classes. It exactly reproduces the zero-noise float baseline because the nonlinear event update and inverse are mathematically perfect. No physical encoder, state-dependent weighted injector, decoder, store or calibration exists. Next identify a circuit topology that can realize signed `f(m+Δm)−f(m)` and subtractive reset within5ms without per-state power/area explosion; otherwise prefer a simpler split-range representation. Keep this software result as a bound, not architecture acceptance.

An eight-range piecewise approximation retains most of the ideal bound: on two full frames it gives2.46/2.55% spike mismatch and87.03/86.14% pixel agreement under the same noise proxy, with exact zero-noise controls. The first-to-last state-voltage slope ratio is~4,496×. Next bench the physical range crossing and charge conservation for a signed event before implementing a larger bank or scaling state inventory. A range switch that changes or loses the preexisting membrane charge is a fail; no software proxy can clear that gate. The existing direct-MOS event source does not implement the required state-dependent charge step.

The first physical10µm+13.3µm differential MIM/TG range bench now passes only a **conditional immediate transfer**: an ideally boundary-precharged extension lets a manually timed positive-current crossing end0.177mV below the ideal piecewise charge calculation. VCM-only extension precharge drops112.5mV stored differential to41.0mV at connection. The ideal matched-precharge branch loses1.184mV/6.82% of its event-minus-reference step over5ms and its unexcited base state falls112.5→104.8mV. The extension target can be a fixed segment-boundary voltage if connection occurs at that boundary; a +1mV ideal-reference error moves final state+0.635mV. Next design and test a physical shared boundary-reference/programming path and reverse boundary crossing, then address5ms leakage/noise before actual float-weight event mapping. No eight-range macro, state-store or PLIF acceptance; retain analog full float and layout gate.

The actual float-weight event-demand check now covers three saved final-fusion sites. The329/322-event near-threshold and cancellation sites request no more than0.858/1.220mV per ideal encoded event, but the370-event maximum-fan-in site requests up to29.9/341.9/245.4mV in forward/reverse/shuffled order;5/16/22 events exceed the measured16.624mV bank-loaded single-event step. The next physical analog experiment should address state-dependent signed charge and a reverse/multiple-range crossing on a modest store, including any extra event service slots, before building reference-programming infrastructure for a per-state capacitor mechanism whose full-range area is already rejected. The current ideal per-record map and one-event ceiling do not qualify a circuit.

An eight-case SS/0°C physical first-event screen now bounds the maximum-drive MOS branch at16.209–17.701mV over differential−1.2 to+1.2V, both event signs, with matched no-event controls. Its9.20% step spread cannot implement the ideal~4,496× piecewise slope change by passive state bias alone. Next explicitly program signed event charge as a function of the analog state or test a shared active integrator, with physical state sensing, gain/reference control, bidirectional crossings and 5ms retention. Keep all readiness gates false.

The aligned, bank-loaded small-drive sequence now shows a concrete signed-balance requirement: at±0.6V stored differential, ten alternating equal pulses leave∓0.131mV versus~0.000042mV at zero. The single positive/negative magnitudes differ by26.3µV at biased state, accumulating through cancellation. Prioritize a physical state-aware sign calibration or differential common-mode feedback before scaling pulse subdivision or claiming high-cancellation weight accuracy. This is still a ten-equal-pulse screen, not actual float-weight fan-in.

Manual sign-specific gate bias0.90116 for the weaker sign, versus0.90000 for the stronger sign, reduces the maximum alternating residual to0.5/0.6µV at−/+0.6V (219–262×). Next measure a state/polarity correction curve at several intermediate states and then feed actual mixed float-weight events through a physical bank with the same state-dependent control law. A controller that knows the state and programs that correction physically has not been built; do not count this as macro acceptance.

The intermediate±0.3V screen also passes nominal equal-pulse cancellation after a half-size0.00058 weaker-sign gate-fraction adjustment: maximum residual0.4/0.3µV versus65.9/65.4µV uncorrected. Next extend this **ideal-source** law across the full encoded voltage span, variable weight amplitudes and event orders. Then build a physical state sensing/programming path and test PVT/mismatch/noise/hold; no full PLIF acceptance.

Area gate supersedes scaling that physical cap-bank cell: the final eight-range slope would require~933pF and~683µm square MIM per side if state capacitance alone supplied the4,496× gain span. Across30.976m states, bare two-cap area is~28.9m². Reject per-state capacitance expansion as the full-range macro. Continue the analog direction by screening state-dependent event-charge scaling on modest storage or a shared active charge-domain representation, while keeping noise, full5ms hold and float semantic replay as gates. The first-boundary switch remains diagnostic only; do not invest in a full per-state eight-cap layout.

The physical first-range actual-weight check now has two distinct outcomes. The329-event near-threshold site sums to within−0.129% to+0.179% across three orders at50.253ns. The322-event high-cancellation site at55ns misses its+5.442µV ideal sum by+11.624/−67.089/−22.995µV; the shorter all-order deck fails solver convergence. An ideal event-by-event state-based polarity command reduces the high-cancellation order spread78.713→46.013µV but still misses, including a shuffled sign reversal. Next obtain a weight/geometry/state-dependent physical transfer calibration and signed cancellation on actual mixed events. A one-geometry equal-pulse bias law is insufficient. Keep analog-only original float, no macro/readiness/layout acceptance.

The mixed-weight high-cancellation source uses all13 geometries (125 events on geometry4) and most effective gate fractions near0.677, far from the corrected equal-pulse geometry0.5/fraction0.9 point. A representative geometry4/fraction0.67683 physical event shows0.67µV polarity imbalance at±30mV state. Shuffle-prefix error reaches−20.58µV by event120 and remains of that order through322. Further local bias fitting is secondary to the still-open independent-state noise, 5ms hold and 30.976m-state storage/area gates; any proposed calibration must be assessed with those gates before macro/layout work.

The bounded SKY130 MOS gate-capacitor screen has a nominal3.14–3.84× capacitance/drawn-gate-area advantage over MIM at0.6–1.2V, but a sampled100µm² gate bank drifts+4.568/−3.726mV at0.6/1.2V over5ms. Its102–113µV independent-pair kT/C proxy and optimistic1,613–1,975mm² gate-only full-state area still fail to qualify precision or density; actual layout area is larger. Next test whether a **shared, sensed, refreshed analog state** can hold off-center values without destroying signed event sums, and account for refresh/service over the full30.976m state inventory. Keep the MOS gate cell as a diagnostic, not a selected macro; do not start full layout or switch away from original full-float analog semantics.

The first shared-refresh service bound rejects **simple repeated full-inventory refresh** as the next layout candidate: a conditional one-pole interpolation of the measured off-center MOS drift needs3.26–4.00µs visits for an illustrative3µV error, implying7.74–9.51million parallel channels at an optimistic1µs/cell. This is not a formal analog tolerance; it excludes read/write, noise and routing. A separate five-voltage TT hold screen finds that a0.6V endpoint-fit exponential misses the0.75V5ms state by258µV, while the1.2V fit misses1.05V by22µV. Characterize that cell at small and large state before physical layout. Keep module and full-chip layout blocked until its noise, 5ms service, cell footprint and PVT results are credible; no placeholder macro or routed-chip claim.

The two-series-TG isolation follow-up does not improve the100µm² MOS gate store: drift remains3.7–4.6mV at±0.3V for every tested middle-node MIM. An ideal-initialized bare gate holds, but an attached off NFET or PFET alone produces millivolt5ms drift, establishing the state-connected off transistors as the immediate leakage path in this nominal model. Do not lay out a simple stacked-TG variant. The next storage/sense candidate needs a different state-node isolation device/bias arrangement or a state encoding with measured noise/hold/semantic tolerance; preserve the original float model. This is a component diagnostic, not a proof that all analog storage is infeasible.

The schematic body-to-state experiment does not establish a repair. Its largest hold movements include a PFET case outside the documented (V_{BS}) range and an NFET case at the boundary; do not use them to select or reject a legal cell. The in-range NFET-at0.6V and PFET-at1.2V cases still move millivolts. The original PDK subset lacked alternative switch models, and official SkyWater SRAM pass devices are restricted to hard-IP arrays. The next viable research step is a supported low-leakage switch/device model with a legal analog layout path **or** a state representation that avoids an individually sampled precision voltage. Run a bounded retention/noise/event update screen before spending on any physical macro. Layout completion cannot be claimed while this core 30.976m-state gate is open.

The TT small-signal noise screen integrates from10Hz to the stated upper bandwidth. The 100× array is not a selected design: it uses about6,000µm² channel area per logical state before routing, while this MIM bank's actual bbox adds12,714µm²/state; the naïve full inventory estimate exceeds0.5m². ### Late-bin comparator autozero failure and next branch — 2026-10-01

The independent “precharge both comparator OTA input gates from a common reference, then connect the membrane and compare” experiment completed for both positive and negative event trains (`scripts/test_sky130_sc_ota_plif_comparator_autozero.py --win=5`). Both decisions had the wrong sign. At the read instant the comparator inputs differed by about−336mV, while the stored membrane differential had been disturbed by−0.645mV. Reject this timing/topology as implemented; it cannot read the existing PLIF bucket without corrupting it. The two-phase same-OTA chopped path remains the better readout candidate: prior ±65µV cases preserve sign with approximately≤0.15µV nominal read disturbance, but a−1.27µV case is misclassified. Next modify the chopped-readout bench to inject calibrated deterministic OTA input offset and use actual float-mapped threshold cases, then measure the offset range that can be canceled without state disturbance and test several timing spacings. Do not layout the comparator or claim analog-neuron acceptance until that microvolt-scale gate passes. Evidence: `sky130_sc_ota_plif_comparator_autozero_{pp,nn}_w5.json` and `sky130_sc_ota_plif_comparator_chopped_{pp,nn,np}_s100n.json`.

### Chopped comparator input-offset stress — 2026-10-01

The two-phase same-OTA comparator was stressed with a deterministic DC source in series with one OTA input, using±100µV and±2.13mV (the latter matches the order of a SKY130 paired ±1σ NFET threshold-offset probe). All four transients completed, but the input stress caused about−43 to−45µV state disturbance even at100µV. With −100µV on the positive-state (`nn`) case the output sign was wrong; at±2.13mV one sign was wrong and the chopped output was dominated by large phase-dependent shifts. This does not emulate a statistical transistor mismatch, but it exposes a failure of the present input-switching topology under modest differential offset: OTA kickback/state coupling rises sharply once offset is present. Do not layout this version. Next isolate the membrane with a buffer or a dedicated sampling capacitor, then repeat the signed offset sweep while monitoring both membrane disturbance and decision residual. Reports: `sky130_sc_ota_plif_comparator_chopped_{pp,nn}_s100n_off100uv.json` and `_off2130uv.json`.

### Temporary isolated sampling and float-margin comparator check — 2026-10-01

The chopped comparator bench now supports a brief state sample onto two temporary MIM capacitors, followed by membrane disconnect before comparison (`--isolated-sample`). With20×20µm sample caps, direct ±3/±6µV programmed differential states survive initialization and a 5ms hold to within about0.42–0.58µV. The nominal ideal-OTA comparator correctly classifies +3/+6µV but misclassifies −3/−6µV, so its zero point is already too asymmetric even with no injected offset. Deterministic±100µV input-offset stress (a stress value, not an OTA mismatch realization) flips the `nn` sign and produces about±2.6µV readout disturbance; at a 65µV event result with zero stress, disturbance is also about±2.6µV due to sampling charge sharing. The 20µm cap pair is approximately0.025m² of added bare MIM over the 30.976m-state inventory and has roughly100µV differential kT/C noise at room temperature by extracted-capacitance scaling. Larger caps reduce that noise at greater area; neither size closes the≈4µV float threshold margin. Next add a physical analog differential offset-calibration path, measure its code range/resolution and calibration retention/noise, and repeat these exact signed-state tests. No comparator/readout layout is justified yet. Reports: `sky130_sc_ota_plif_comparator_chopped_{pp,nn}_s100n_d{3,6}uv_isolated_c20u.json` and corresponding negative-state/offset reports.

### PDK mismatch realization on the chopped comparator — 2026-10-01

Applied the SKY130 TT PDK's published independent ±1σ Vth slopes directly to the comparator OTA's two input NFET model instances. For a held +6µV differential, both OTA phases rail near1.797V, the chopped delta is only−3.07µV, and the decision is wrong. The membrane itself moves about−0.226µV during readout, so the failure is the comparator's offset/decision function rather than state kickback. This is one deterministic paired-sigma realization, not Monte Carlo or yield, and uses schematic switches with no extracted readout. It decisively rejects the symmetric ideal-OTA comparator without offset calibration. Next test a per-neuron analog stored-offset correction with PDK mismatch, exact ±3/±6µV states and both mismatch polarities; include trim retention, kT/C and service/area before moving toward any comparator layout. Evidence: `sky130_sc_ota_plif_comparator_chopped_pp_s100n_d6uv_isolated_c20u_pdkvth1sigma.json`.

### Comparator OTA trim-range characterization — 2026-10-01

A DC transfer sweep of the exact0.5µm-input SKY130 comparator OTA, using the PDK's independent paired ±1σ NFET Vth slopes, finds the output crossing at+9.474mV for one assignment and−9.534mV after swapping the mismatch signs (1µV refinement around each crossing). Local gain is approximately−3,250V/V. This implies roughly±10mV analog correction range; to leave≤2µV ideal quantization residual over20mV span would require about14 effective bits. A transient with an ideal−9.474mV correction did not restore the +6µV chopped decision: both output phases remained near the low rail and the differential decision was wrong. Thus the DC crossing is useful range data, not a working trim. The source-based trim transient does not implement a DAC or stored offset and its phase operating point does not match the DC transfer well enough. Next diagnose transient common-mode/settling under trimmed PDK mismatch, then implement a switched physical trim/sample path and remeasure ±3/±6µV for both mismatch assignments. Keep all readout and full-chip layout blocked. Evidence: `scripts/characterize_sky130_sc_ota_vth_offset.py`, its `_c9p47m_s0p05m_d1u` / `_swapped_c-9p53m_s0p05m_d1u` reports, and `sky130_sc_ota_plif_comparator_chopped_pp_s100n_d6uv_isolated_c20u_pdkvth1sigma_trim-9474uv.json`.

### Active bucket corner and reference-offset extension — 2026-10-02

The near-threshold active-bucket replay was repeated at SKY130 SS/0°C and FF/85°C with the TT-calibrated shared packet gain0.78. The expected full-float incremental state is+32.623µV; the measured signal-minus-no-event-reference changes are+31.2µV at SS/0°C, +31.6µV at TT/27°C, and+29.2µV at FF/85°C. Their errors are−1.42,−1.02, and−3.42µV, respectively. The event contribution stays correct-sign, but the FF error approaches the selected site's≈4.09µV float threshold margin. The absolute no-event OTA output is0.894V at SS/0°C and0.413V at FF/85°C at5ms (versus0.9V VCM); the differential readout only appears stable because a matched reference branch is subtracted.

A deterministic+0.1mV perturbation to the neutral reference OTA's bias source moves the near-threshold event-minus-neutral result to+0.676mV, versus+31.7µV with zero offset. This is not a statistical mismatch model, but it shows that the present reference subtraction cannot be trusted without a physical correlated reference/offset calibration path. Continue with a differential active bucket and physical common-mode/offset control; the current single-ended OTA bucket is not ready for layout release or full-float acceptance. Reports are listed under `physical_active_bucket_pvt_screen` and `physical_active_bucket_reference_offset_stress` in the architecture boundary JSON.



### Complementary active-bucket common-mode probe — 2026-10-02

A new two-bucket complementary-packet simulation was run on the verified epoch58 float near-threshold fan-in (329 events, TT/27°C,5ms). Common mode stays near0.8983V, but the half-difference is only0.093µV versus32.623µV target; the current pair setup is rejected. The single-bucket charge result does not transfer automatically to a differential implementation. An isolated largest-positive event also produces only0.038µV half-difference for a0.801µV target, while both branches shift together by≈0.49mV common mode. Debug the paired-cell stimulus/reset/connect sequence and pin/sign conventions before retrying the full-float sequence. Do not start an active-bucket macro layout until differential transfer, threshold decision, noise/mismatch and extracted behavior pass.

### Complementary active bucket phase correction and float replay — 2026-10-02

The first differential-pair failure was a runner timing bug: the packet-connect switch overlapped packet-reset release. The passing single-bucket deck uses a dead interval, then a later connect window. Matching that exact phase sequence restores transfer. One positive event measures0.785µV half-difference against0.801µV target. Full near-threshold epoch58 float fan-in (329 events, gain0.78) errors are+1.135µV forward,+0.137µV reverse,+0.515µV shuffle at TT; SS/0°C shuffle is+0.259µV and FF/85°C shuffle−0.675µV. The same near-threshold shuffle finishes at common mode0.894V SS,0.868V TT,0.413V FF. High-cancellation shuffle error is+0.451µV for a7.5nV target; max-fanin shuffle error is+0.247mV for−52.454mV target. These remain ideal packet-driver schematic results, without threshold/readout, mismatch/noise, extraction, or layout.

A first transistor CMFB driving the OTA load-bias node held FF common mode near0.9005V but collapsed differential state to1.56µV; reversing its input sign drove the outputs to ground. Moving its control to weak output PFET assist left FF common mode at0.417V and made differential error+0.212mV. Reject these CMFB versions. The paired differential bucket is a viable schematic charge-transfer lead, not a qualified neuron. Next quantify full-float threshold decisions using the paired output, determine whether common-mode range is compatible with a physical comparator at FF, then implement a physical event-weight driver and extracted OTA/MIM macro after its noise/mismatch gates. Reports and runner are named in `physical_active_bucket_complementary_pair_phase_corrected` in the architecture boundary JSON and `scripts/test_sky130_sc_integrator_differential_pair.py`.

### Differential-bucket threshold latch across corners — 2026-10-02

Connected the complementary active-bucket output to the existing SKY130 regenerative latch, using an ideal differential threshold source set from the original epoch58 float threshold. With a5ns transient maximum, the TT near-threshold case resolves to the correct no-spike output; the measured bucket event increment is32.073µV against32.623µV target. At FF/85°C, the same untrimmed latch resolves to the opposite polarity (false spike) even though the bucket event increment is32.036µV, within0.59µV of target. Applying ideal threshold shifts of+2.5µV,+3.75µV and+5µV brackets the FF latch crossing:3.75µV still misclassifies;5µV classifies this selected case correctly. This only establishes a corner-specific offset target; it is not a physical trim, mismatch distribution, noise, or full-margin decision pass.

A 20µm physical bottom-plate MIM/TG level-shift sampler raises the FF latch input common mode to about0.900V, but its off-state loading shifts the bucket event result by roughly0.590mV over the5ms bin. Its sampled input is about−208µV, far from the expected few-microvolt decision residual. Reject this sampler as implemented. Next build a low-common-mode-compatible regenerative input stage or a buffered/trimmed readout, characterize its offset over float-margin distribution and PVT, then return to extracted macro layout. The active-bucket charge core still has no physical weight encoder or qualified initial-state loader; do not claim PLIF acceptance or full-chip layout. Evidence is in `physical_active_bucket_threshold_latch_screen` in the boundary JSON and the referenced result reports.

Continue the SKY130 full-float path. At those corners, bucket increment errors are−0.460/−0.560µV and added read disturbance is≤0.030µV. The same fixed pair fails at SS/0°C; the unassisted latch also false-spikes at SS. These are schematic, 5ns-step screens using an ideal float-derived threshold, with no mismatch/noise or physical trim.

Next implement a real differential offset correction or auto-zero input path and calibrate against a distribution of original-float threshold margins, then characterize offset/noise across PVT. A 0.1× device sizing run was invalid because its 0.2µm PFET is below the SKY130 model minimum; do not treat it as a circuit result.


### Full-float comparator margin budget — 2026-10-02

Analyzed all1,966,080 final-fusion candidate values in the verified epoch58 float trace. Under the current exploratory SKY130 mapping, absolute margin quantiles are0.537µV at the0.1 percentile,5.28µV at1%, and54.5µV at5%;0.20% of all trace values lie within1µV of threshold and0.95% within5µV. Among float spikes, the0.1-percentile margin is0.264µV. These are mapped sensitivity values, not an accepted physical scaling, and the trace covers sampled patches rather than full-dataset process validation. They show the selected ±5µV trim experiment is far too narrow to certify readout accuracy: physical comparator offset/noise must be characterized against the complete margin histogram, followed by full-float replay with measured error. The diagnostic script is `scripts/analyze_sky130_float_threshold_margin_distribution.py`; JSON is `sky130_float_threshold_margin_distribution.json`.


A zero-mean independent Gaussian perturbation applied analytically to these saved margins predicts0.077% state-decision flips at1µV RMS (0.146% of float spikes) and0.375% at5µV RMS (0.693% of float spikes). This is a sensitivity calculation only; it excludes temporal/systematic correlations, neuron feedback, downstream propagation and task accuracy. Use it to set circuit noise/offset characterization points, not as an analog simulation result.


### Full-frame comparator-noise and nonlinear-scale replay — 2026-10-02

Extended the epoch58 full-float replay with independent voltage-equivalent noise at every PLIF threshold decision. This is a software sensitivity test, not measured PDK noise. With a per-actor linear ±0.9V mapping and0.1µV RMS threshold noise across all actors, one complete 440×640 validation frame has1.68–1.93% final-fuse spike mismatch and89.4–90.6% pixel agreement across three seeds; labeled accuracy changes from49.82% float to49.50–49.99%. At0.25µV RMS, pixel agreement is86.2–86.9% and accuracy49.16–49.73%.

An ideal asinh mapping at range gain1000 improves one-frame pixel agreement to97.14–98.61% under0.1µV noise. An ideal 8-segment piecewise approximation at the same gain gives99.972–99.994% agreement on record0 and99.21–99.99% on record1; full-float labeled accuracy stays within about0.05 percentage points of each record's baseline across tested seeds. This suggests that nonlinear state scaling can make comparator noise less damaging without quantizing the model. It does not validate the encoding hardware: the piecewise map, range sensing/switching, charge updates and retention are ideal, and the noise is synthetic. Continue by implementing a physical range-coded analog state update and repeat the full-frame replay with measured transient/mismatch errors before layout. JSON evidence is `sky130_full_float_threshold_noise_replay_boundary.json` and `full_float_threshold_noise_sensitivity_replay` in the architecture boundary.

Tested larger clocked PMOS input pairs on the same full-float near-threshold fan-in at SS/0°C, with a5ns transient step. Increasing complementary device scale from the minimum legal0.21× to0.5× and1× did not correct the false-spike decision. The active bucket still tracks the target within0.075/0.170µV; latch sampled input differences remain−8.31/−8.01µV and both resolve to the wrong polarity. Further fixed pair sizing is not the useful next step. Proceed with a physical analog offset calibration/trim mechanism and compare its calibrated input-referred residual to the original-float margin distribution; then characterize noise and PVT. A subsequent ideal threshold trim diagnostic at1× complementary-pair size applied−10µV and restored the selected SS no-spike decision; bucket error stayed0.082µV and read disturbance0.031µV. This is a trim-range hint only. It does not establish physical trim resolution, calibration stability, other neuron margins, PVT yield or noise. Use it to size a physical analog calibration experiment; the mandatory residual-noise target still comes from the full-float margin distribution (q0.1≈0.537µV under the exploratory mapping).
### Readout range continuation — 2026-10-02

The regenerative latch's large-signal polarity failure was traced to its auxiliary clocked PMOS input pair, not an ASIC-tool blockage. Removing that pair produces correct max-fanin/no-spike decisions at TT and FF on the actual 370-event full-float trace; the corresponding event-transfer errors are about+4µV TT and+2.83mV FF, so FF state accuracy still fails. The SS max-fanin transient currently aborts on a timestep/convergence error at the ideal threshold source. Small-margin decisions still fail at SS/FF without calibration. A 4:1 switched resistive attenuator (source TG isolates the bucket outside the read window) passes max-fanin TT and near-threshold TT, but it shrinks the near-threshold input at FF below the latch's offset; high-cancellation TT also false-spikes. Reject fixed attenuation as a complete readout.

Next keep the NMOS-only latch as the range-capable lead and test shared/per-lane readout or non-sampled analog offset cancellation. An optimistic independent MIM sample-and-hold offset trim needs about860.7m² bare MIM across the membrane inventory to meet the exploratory q0.1 mapped margin; reject per-identity sampled MIM trim. Diagnose SS convergence on high-fanin traces. A 1MΩ threshold-reference series resistor and Gear integration did not clear the SS failure; both still stop at `vthbias#branch` with an extremely small timestep. Do not start readout macro layout until corrected full-float decisions, retained state accuracy, and read disturbance pass SS/TT/FF; state store, physical weight delivery, and 5ms system service remain separate gates. Exploratory PMOS preamp screen was misbiased (tail remained near VDD, output common mode under0.5mV) and is rejected. Evidence: `sky130_autozero_noise_area_boundary.json`, `sky130_sc_integrator_diffpair_max_fanin_full_shuffle_{tt,ff}_latch*.json`, `sky130_sc_integrator_diffpair_near_threshold_scale_full_shuffle_{tt,ff}_latch*.json`, `sky130_sc_integrator_diffpair_high_cancellation_full_shuffle_tt_latch*.json`, `sky130_regenerative_latch_input_range*.json`, and `sky130_monotonic_readout_preamplifier.json`.

The measured2.6µs latch resolve time implies at least16,108 parallel comparators to visit the30,976,000 states within5ms, with zero allowance for mux or control time. Next compare a fast shared OTA/preamplifier with a differential-chopped output against that service floor and measured offset/noise; do not assume the existing underbiased PMOS preamp is reusable.
