# Current analog ASIC gate — shared differential state/readout


Passive gate feedback was screened and rejected: it misses corner common mode and leaks a1mV differential state to zero by1ms. Simple OTA sizing/compensation changes also fail zero-event hold. Next test a linear low-gain common-mode controller that senses the output mean without loading the differential state, or move to another active differential state/readout topology. Reusable layout remains gated on5ms PVT hold and full-float update fidelity.

Input-pair source degeneration (100Ω–10kΩ) plus compensation and tail-bias sweeps did not stabilize all corners; best sampled TT=0.927V still collapses SS and misses FF. Continue with a structurally bounded/linear CMFB implementation, retaining shared differential state without resistive loading. Do not layout this candidate yet.

Update: direct differential switched-capacitor membrane state has passed schematic transient sign, cancellation and4.99ms retention at TT/SS/FF. It uses paired storage capacitors plus opposite switched charge packets, so it avoids the failed continuous common-mode servo. Next build a reusable paired-cell layout and qualify extracted PEX across corners. Then map actual epoch58 float event fan-in and measured threshold/readout margins. Keep full-chip assembly gated on those module checks; preserve analog/full-float direction.

### 2026-10-02 extracted packet cell and float-order gate

The direct MIM packet path now uses DRC/LVS-qualified layout cells for the paired state capacitor (3x4 array,21.84pF nominal per rail) and2x2µm packet capacitor, plus extracted SKY130 TG PEX. With10 parallel reset TGs, two state-bank copies per rail measured a0.123mV/0.3V packet transfer at SS and0.095mV/0.3V at FF. These are cell-level PEX results; top-level wiring remains absent.

Replayed the selected epoch58 float near-threshold fan-in (321 events, trace `[17,7,1,26]`, candidate margin≈2.15nV under the current VPU map) in forward/shuffle/reverse order using matched TT calibration. One bank gives final errors+3.12/−0.72/−5.61µV; two banks give+1.66/−0.36/−2.97µV; four banks give+0.78/−0.24/−1.54µV. Four-bank reverse was checked at both50ns and100ns maximum timestep; the final values differ by≈0.04µV. The order span falls with capacitance, but remains far above this trace's decision margin. Four differential banks also imply≈3.17m² bare capacitor bounding-box area over30,976,000 states (one bank≈0.79m²), before switches or routing. Do not scale this MIM implementation further as the state architecture.

Keep this packet cell as a qualified primitive, not the selected membrane implementation. Continue the lower-area physical MOS-cap state path with the signed pulse-duration injector, replacing ideal state feedback/bias commands and resolving5ms retention and refresh service. The physical MOS-cap layouts exist; event control, state sensing/feedback, precision across the float-margin distribution, and population service are still unqualified. Do not start macro/full-chip assembly until those gates pass. Keep the direct packet results and all analog artifacts for comparison; no quantized/QAT reference is used.

### Latest continuation — VCM-corrected MOS pair and float replay — 2026-10-02

The paired100×10µm MOS-cap layout now has separate differential gate rails and a common VCM connection; it passes DRC/LVS and the extracted AC characterization covers the actual VCM-centered state range. The old standalone capacitance/noise numbers used channel=0V and were not representative: at zero state, measured C is14.45pF/rail; reset-noise proxy is15.37µV RMS. Continue using only the corrected VCM-centered values for system analysis.

Best current state-storage candidate is the100×10µm per-device bank, not the20×2µm variant. The smaller bank lowers the bare two-rail area estimate to≈0.30m² but its measured 5ms drift produces only≈91.9% full-frame pixel agreement, even under an ideal state map. The100×10µm bank projects≈0.76m², close to the single MIM bank, but two sampled full-float frames retain≈96.7–96.8% pixel agreement and≈0.49–0.50% final-fuse spike mismatch after measured retention at SS; record0 TT/FF replays are similar. Do not call this an accepted state store: event injection, range-aware drive/encoding, physical readout, transient noise/mismatch, routing and array service remain missing.

Next: map the extracted pair C(V) and hold curves into signed original-float event fan-in with a physically realizable state sensor/driver, then include threshold/reset/β and run full-frame replay using measured error. Separately estimate area and service for the actual dual-rail macro. If those gates cannot meet the project’s float-fidelity target, return to the shared active bucket plus another retained-state element; do not scale the20×2µm bank or the MIM packet array.


### Shared-reset timing and reset-noise replay — 2026-10-02

Opening the inter-rail bridge while both VCM clamps remain on reduces the deterministic differential kick in the extracted TG/pair transient from 1.243µV to 0.132nV. The rails stay clamped for another 10µs before release. This is a sequencing result only: the deterministic simulator has no thermal noise or mismatch, and the independent-reset comparator also remains near zero in that model. It does not establish correlated reset noise. Reports and deck are `sky130_mos_pair_correlated_reset_ss0` and `sky130_mos_pair_correlated_reset_clamp_last_ss0`.

Replayed one full DSEC frame from the verified epoch58 full-float checkpoint with measured TT 5ms pair retention, ideal piecewise8 state encoding, and illustrative independent 24µV RMS differential reset noise. Three seeds with noise only at frame initialization gave 99.931–99.939% pixel agreement at range gain100, 99.918–99.919% at1000, and99.864% at10000. Reapplying the same noise after every subtractive spike reset gave99.927–99.935%,99.918–99.919%, and99.863–99.865%, respectively. Final-fusion spike mismatch remained below0.10%; labeled pixel accuracy remained near the float baseline49.82%. These results show the previous conclusion that 24µV reset noise necessarily destroys frame fidelity was tied to a less suitable state map/gain case. This replay remains a sensitivity test: piecewise encoding/update is ideal, the Gaussian is not a PDK transient-noise waveform, and the single frame does not qualify the circuit.

Next characterize the physical noise and state-dependent error of the extracted event injector against the original-float high-cancellation, maximum-fanin and near-threshold sequences. Build/extract a reset-and-state macro only after the injector and readout topology are credible; then run PVT/noise and replay measured errors. No full PLIF or chip acceptance is claimed.


### Extracted float-weight MOS injector sequence gate — 2026-10-02

Replayed the 322-event epoch58 high-cancellation final-fusion fan-in through the extracted SKY130 ten-device MOS state banks and 13-geometry signed injector at SS/0°C. With the existing ideal per-event bias commands, 4.4µs transfer pulses and 5µs slots, the shuffled full sequence ends at −49.77µV versus the full-float target +5.442µV: −55.22µV error and a final sign mismatch. The equivalent 40-event prefix ends −6.430µV from target; 2.5ns and10ns maximum timesteps agree within0.003µV, establishing numerical convergence for that prefix. A simple behavioral bidirectional state-feedback slope of0.004/V worsens the full shuffled error to−63.81µV. Measured-state-transfer correction on the first40 events worsens error to−102.56µV, while the ideal polarity correction leaves it near−6.59µV. These results do not support taking the current long-pulse MOS injector into a PLIF layout. They are bounded one-site analog screens, without physical weight DAC, mismatch/noise, threshold/reset/β or full network. Evidence is in `sky130_ms_mos_state_injector_boundary_high_cancellation_shuffle_layoutbank_dt10.json`, `_n40_dt2p5.json`, `_n40_dt10.json`, the state-feedback report and `_n40` correction reports.

Continue with the active differential charge bucket / switched-capacitor event path as the leading analog alternative. First characterize full-float weighted event order, near-threshold output, physical weight delivery, PVT, readout and retention together; reuse existing DRC/LVS-clean primitive cells where applicable. Do not scale the current MOS injector or claim a selected neuron macro yet.

2026-10-03 dual-reservoir continuation: physically extracted positive/negative passive MIM reservoirs with PEX packet cells and PEX TGs were replayed on the original322-event full-float fan-in. 2026-10-03 continuation: Use fixed-width amplitude-coded injection as the next analog event-source candidate after the pulse-duration, passive packet and OTA-C paths failed their sequence gates. State density, threshold readout and service remain the system-level layout blockers.

2026-10-03 static geometry weight follow-up: the segmented runner had been restarting each chunk at the DC operating point; its apparent 23% full-sequence error is invalid as state-carry evidence. Correct UIC starts exposed rapid off-state loading in the direct geometry bank: with a 100µm state cap the differential moved 2.90mV during the 90ns before the first event. A per-branch series NMOS access switch reduced that pre-event change to 34nV, and a polarity-reversed, 5× widened single-event case delivered +0.695mV against +0.683mV target (1.81% error) at SS/0°C. This is one isolated event only: the 20-event fan-in deck fails because the largest geometry-scaled MOS exceeds the PDK model range, and an attempted 5-finger instance aborts at transient startup. Do not treat the geometry bank as an accepted accumulator. Corrected scripts now require the ngspice completion marker and reject transient aborts, and reports record requested initial state voltages. Next measure an in-range isolated-access transfer grid and off-state retention, then replay ordered float fan-in only if both pass; integrated layout remains gated.

Follow-up qualification boundary (2026-10-03): with in-range geometry widths and a PEX-style series transmission gate, the one-event sweep at headroom0.2–0.9 delivers only34–20% of the float event charge (66–80% low). Widening the transmission gate from10 to40µm improves one-event charge from0.115mV to0.357mV against0.683mV, while common mode shifts up to2.7mV. A prior 5× raw MOS width case matched one event but exceeded the PDK geometry range in the full-fanin deck; reject that apparent scaling solution. Instantiating all360 branches was killed by the bounded Docker memory limit, and a33-branch attempt hit timestep underflow at the event edge. These are simulator/bench limits plus an actual underdriven event; no full sequence or integrated layout passes. Next proceed with a convergent isolated-switch driver or the shared calibrated injector physical weight-source build, preserving the analog full-float reference.

Series-isolated pulse-width control check (2026-10-03): the first float event was sampled1ns after the pulse at10.5/30/52.5ns. Measured differential increments were0.230/0.217/0.202mV against+0.683mV target (−66.4/−68.3/−70.4%); common mode shifted+0.69mV. This confirms the in-range underdrive is not caused by the former100ns observation delay, and extending pulse width makes transfer slightly worse. Preserve full-float event source and investigate a differently biased current injector or shared physical DAC path; do not layout the isolated geometry cell.

2026-10-03 shared analog bias-source continuation: performed TT/SS/FF switched-event checks with TT-trimmed 12-bit R-2R codes on the actual PDK MOS/TG injector gates and extracted 10um MIM state pair. Events were 42.2657ns and used shared 0.85/0.90/0.95 gate rails; event-charge error was −2.32..−3.21% TT, −4.64..−5.97% SS, and −1.44..−2.01% FF. 2026-10-03 routed resistor ladder continuation: built `sky130_physical_r2r2_slice` from seven xhigh-poly PCells and metal1 routes. DRC=0, unique LVS, GDS present. PDK MOS multiplicity screens give worst step variation 28.7% at10x,10.3% at100x,1.32% at1000x; the large variants are schematic-only and omit their physical interconnect parasitics. Next implement a physically sized high-drive TG array and rerun routed extraction and event settling, then use the combined ladder/TG transfer in full-float replay. 2026-10-03 resistor-drive continuation: model-only resistor-length scaling (1–50×) leaves17.99% worst step deviation at SS/0°C even at50×. Combined bench output is still nonuniform (4-cell worst98.97%, 32-cell worst59.64%), even with the bank's caps removed; line folding did not change it. ### 2026-10-03 routed state-pair update and current analog gate

The two-rail SKY130 MIM state pair now has a routed GDS, zero Magic DRC, unique Netgen LVS, and a five-capacitor interconnect PEX. The macro area projects to about0.984m² if its present bounding box is replicated for all30,976,000 state identities. It contains state capacitors and a shared VCM strap only.

The composite pair PEX was inserted into the epoch58 full-float 321-event near-threshold replay at TT, with forward/shuffle/reverse order, and SS/FF shuffle. TT errors are+2.374/−1.179/−5.718µV; SS and FF shuffle each end−1.176µV from float. The tested float margin is+2.146nV, so only TT forward preserves the arithmetic decision. The packet-amplitude commands remain ideal and the test has no comparator, noise, mismatch or physical weight DAC. This closes physical storage-pair layout and its bounded PEX replay, not neuron qualification.

**Next:** retain this cell as a primitive. Investigate a smaller analog state element and an event-transfer method with substantially lower order/state-dependent error. Continue to gate neuron and 140k assembly on original-float weighted traces, threshold/reset/β, actual readout, 5ms retention, noise/mismatch and 5ms population service. No digital or QAT fallback.

**2026-10-03 continuation:** corrected the compact20×2µm paired MOS-cap layout and qualified its DRC/LVS/extracted AC; extracted-TG retention with the inactive injector attached is0.55–0.57µV drift from±3µV and1.11–1.13µV from±6µV across TT/SS/FF at27°C. This pair reduces the projected two-rail bounding-box area to≈0.312m² but raises ideal differential kT/C to112µV RMS. Keep it as a negative trade-off result, not the selected state element. The initial seven-state runs exceeded the5-minute bound; focused four-state runs completed. Next pursue first-touch/zero-state clamping and common-mode-correlated reset against actual sampled-noise models, while retaining original-float update replay as the acceptance gate. Integrated neuron/full-chip layout remains unqualified.

10×1µm pair continuation: extracted DRC/LVS/AC passes, with0.194pF per rail,206.8µV ideal differential kT/C and≈0.171m² all-state bbox. Attached-injector/extracted-TG +3µV hold leaves+1.54–1.58µV after5ms across TT/SS/FF. This smaller size worsens both deterministic error and noise; reject it. Continue only with a physically grounded way to avoid independent reset noise (first-touch or correlated reference/reset) and a readout/update circuit that passes the frozen full-float sequences. Current layout artifacts are standalone primitives; the 140k hierarchical assembly is gated.

**Reset noise gate:** PEX `.noise` for the100×10µm paired MOS state plus TG is21.73/20.31/23.83µV RMS at TT/SS/FF. A shared bridge reduces it by at most1.8%; single-ended MOS storage measures15.36/14.36/16.85µV at the larger size and60.76–113.99µV at smaller sizes. One-TG shared-reset switching also leaves≈0.8–1.3µV after reset and drifts to as much as55µV by5ms at FF; matched bridge/clamp release is worse. Reject the tested common-bridge reset as a noise fix. Next test a different low-noise analog state/readout topology or quantify achievable precision with a physically correlated state/reference pair. All values are small-signal or deterministic PEX screens, not transient-noise/yield results. Full details and runners are in the latest `docs/RESULTS.md` section.

Existing no-QAT full-frame replay at12.9715µV state noise gives≈10% final-fusion spike mismatch and mean IoU0.128–0.134 versus0.2057 float for record0. The measured15–17µV single-ended100×10µm PEX noise is already above this failing probe, before readout noise. Keep the ideal analog single-ended state mapping out of acceptance. The state/noise gate remains open.

Additional full-float check at15.3646µV after each subtractive reset confirms that ideal `asinh` and ideal8-segment maps do not rescue fidelity: both yield≈10.48% final-fusion spike mismatch,≈64.6% pixel agreement and≈0.127 mean IoU versus0.20573 float. These mapping functions are not physical circuits. Next route a non-capacitive analog state candidate if one can retain local state without sampled noise; otherwise pause layout promotion at the primitive stage until a physically viable store/readout method is found. No decision from the user is required to reject these measured storage candidates.

2026-10-03 R-2R/TG composition debug: screened the extracted 4- and 32-device TG banks with only the selected rail attached and common ideal gate drives. At 32 devices per bit/rail, the extracted resistor ladder remains monotonic with 3.76/18.27/1.27% maximum equal-step deviation at TT/27C, SS/0C, FF/85C; four devices give 15.70/26.24/8.23%. Keeping all off-bank devices attached causes materially worse nonlinearity, so the prior all-branch composition is invalid for a one-of-two rail-select topology. A schematic closed-loop OTA follower per bit input composed with the 32-device bank and extracted ladder completed DC analyses but produced 41.00/141.16/40.50% maximum step deviation; the SS code3 output also required convergence stepping. Reject that follower composition pending a minimal ideal-buffer control and bias/topology audit. Scripts/results: `scripts/sim_sky130_parallel_tg_r2r2.py`, `scripts/sim_sky130_buffered_tg_r2r2.py`, `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_parallel_tg_r2r2/parallel_tg_r2r2_n4_selected_only_tied_controls.json`, `.../parallel_tg_r2r2_n32_selected_only_tied_controls.json`, `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_buffered_tg_r2r2/buffered_tg_r2r2.json`. Correction (2026-10-03): the first OTA-follower deck omitted the ladder's VLO reference, invalidating its earlier 41/141/40% numbers. The corrected deck includes the driven VLO pin. The ideal-buffer control reproduces 25mV uniform TT steps within0.0042%, confirming the reference and ladder pin order. The corrected OTA follower still fails: max step deviation is21.80% TT,78.87% SS; the FF code3 output is141.73V, outside supply rails and a failed operating point despite ngspice's completion marker. The result is diagnostic only and the buffer is rejected. Runner now records a rail-bound/transfer-qualified gate. Continue with a stable analog buffer or use the physical TG bank directly only if its SS 18.27% step error can be calibrated by a physically realized analog trim. Independent membrane precision/readout gates remain unresolved.

2026-10-03 corrected analog-buffer conclusion: rerunning the control with the ladder reference explicitly tied reproduces the extracted ideal code transfer; correcting the OTA deck removes the floating-reference artifact but reveals genuine OTA failure at SS/FF. The 32-TG direct transfer can be fit by a shared affine analog gain/offset with maximum residual0.598mV TT,2.158mV SS,0.189mV FF; SS range is only58.17mV versus71.77mV TT across four codes. This is a diagnostic fit to simulated outputs, not a realized trim. A single global gain/offset would leave multi-mV nonlinear SS error and cannot satisfy nV-scale tested float margins. No QAT or digital weight fallback is proposed. The memory/readout/update precision gates remain the controlling blocker.

2026-10-03 analog DAC buffer progress: increased the OTA output stage to wn3=20µm/wp3=100µm and used corner-specific analog bias values (TT/FF0.65/0.75V; SS0.55/0.65V). This is an analog bias calibration, not weight quantization. The circuit is not qualified: its schematic OTA buffer has no physical layout/PEX, and an extracted-TG transient with ideal gate edges drifts by several millivolts during each5µs hold. Runners/reports: `scripts/sim_sky130_buffered_tg_r2r2.py`, `scripts/test_sky130_buffered_r2r2_settling.py`, `scripts/sweep_sky130_ota_follower_bias.py`, and `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_buffered_tg_r2r2*/`.

2026-10-03 matched-corner companding sensitivity: replayed the verified epoch58 original full-float checkpoint on full DSEC frames for records0 and1 with all-neuron independent24µV RMS state noise at initialization and after each subtractive spike reset, preserving the subtractively reset state, plus the measured SKY130 SS/0°C differential MOS-bank/TG retention profile. The ideal six-segment asinh map at per-actor gain1000 gives record0 mean pixel agreement87.02% and mean IoU0.20592 versus float0.20573; record1 gives86.08% pixel agreement and0.20089 mean IoU versus0.19892. Previous priority was a physically realizable six-range analog state map around the routed MIM/TG primitives. The active-buffered capacitor transition has now failed continuity at the proposed upper boundary, so stop developing that exact range-switch path. The current direction is a fixed MIM state capacitor with state-range-selected analog event gain, preserving the original full-float weights through pulse duration and avoiding storage-bank reconnection. Preserve six-segment full-float replay only as a candidate accuracy target; require physical signed weighted events, boundary crossing, 5ms hold/noise, original-float replay, threshold/reset/beta and5ms population service before reusable neuron layout. Full-chip assembly remains gated. No QAT/digital fallback.

2026-10-03 active MIM extension precharge prototype: added `scripts/test_sky130_active_mim_range_precharge.py` and ran SKY130 TT/27C differential MIM/TG transient in bounded Docker/ngspice. The circuit uses the PDK OTA as a state-following precharge buffer (`inp=buffer output`, `inn=live state`, required by this OTA's inverting output polarity), isolates the extension, then attaches it to the fine state bank. The simulation completes, but this circuit does not preserve state to the required precision: extension differential differs from live state by+330.9µV; attachment shifts the state by−281.7µV at10ns and−289.0µV after300ns; final event branch is−365.4µV from the matched no-transition reference. Buffer output differential tracks live differential to−70.1µV before the precharge TG opens. This is a schematic nominal transient without mismatch/noise/PVT/PEX; reject this buffer+TG implementation as a qualified range switch. The first bench attempt had invalid `meas` expressions; after fixing the vector syntax, measurements passed. A temporary input-polarity reversal drove both buffer outputs near6.9mV and extension differential to≈0, confirming it is the wrong feedback polarity. Next test charge-domain/reference-assisted extension transfer and clock-edge/dummy-switch cancellation; no six-range or neuron layout promotion until a measured boundary passes.

Expanded the same runner to test differential state spans of±1mV and±50mV. The transition error is nearly odd-symmetric: at±1mV it is≈±3.5µV relative to the no-transition reference, at±50mV≈±162µV, and at+112.5mV−365µV. Slowing all control edges from0.1ns to5ns and25ns leaves the large-signal final error around−363µV, so edge slew alone does not correct it. This nonlinear/signal-dependent error makes the buffer unsuitable across the proposed state ranges, even before variation. Machine reports use `sky130_active_mim_range_precharge_tt_edge*ns_diff*mv.json`.

Tested OTA sizing/bias and partial-swing switch drive on the 112.5mV and300mV spans. Widening the OTA input pair10→40/100µm did not improve settled continuity and greatly increased state/reference deviation. With1.6V/0.2V TG on-levels, a50mV state leaves−15.1µV relative to reference, but112.5mV leaves−118.2µV and300mV leaves−571.2µV using VBP=0.65V. At1.55V the extension fails to precharge (−19.65mV mismatch) and state drifts−12.16mV; at1.62V the300mV point remains−0.90mV from reference. Lower VBP=0.55/0.65/0.75 at300mV with1.6V switches gives final errors−523/−571/−659µV, respectively. This is not a robust six-range transition; amplitude and bias tuning cannot address the high-boundary error. Candidate pivot: keep one fixed state capacitor and move the piecewise gain into the event packet path, selected continuously by state range. That removes state-cap bank reconnection; it still needs physical signed weighted event, boundary crossing, precision, retention, and throughput tests before layout promotion.

Sweep scripts: `scripts/test_sky130_active_mim_range_precharge.py` (optional CLI: edge ns, state differential mV, OTA input width µm, PMOS bias V, TG on-high V); `scripts/summarize_sky130_active_mim_precharge.py` aggregates completed cases into `artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_active_mim_precharge_sweep_summary.json`. The sweep contains22 valid transient cases. It does not certify the active-buffer topology.

Range selector timing seed: added `scripts/test_sky130_piecewise_range_comparator.py`. A SKY130 TT PDK comparator at VCM0.9V, loaded by30fF, switches high7.879ns after an ideal20ns state ramp crosses the first positive +150mV boundary. This is one schematic comparator point; no PEX, PVT/mismatch/noise, full range decoder or coupled injector. A startup edge initially confused the measurement; the runner now measures the second rising threshold crossing and confirms low output before, high output after. Next compose the comparator output with a fixed-MIM gain-selected signed pulse injector and measure switching charge/error on a real boundary-crossing event.

Next fixed-cap event-path sizing evidence: ran `scripts/analyze_sky130_piecewise_actual_event_range.py --range-gain=1000 --segments=6` against three actual original-float final-fusion event multisets. Near-threshold(329) and high-cancellation(322) events request encoded steps with medians≈19µV and maxima≈111/157µV; each has two events below the measured injector floor. Max-fanin(370) has0.87/5.84/9.66mV median/90th/max forward and1.86/10.38/112.54mV shuffled, with28/370 shuffled events above the measured single-event ceiling and55 optimistic extra equal-ceiling subslots(≈2.3µs at42.27ns slots). These are ideal piecewise targets compared with one measured SS/0°C injector ceiling, not physical delivery. The event counts previously recorded as range crossings combined range changes with zero crossings; corrected split: near-threshold has0 true range changes and1/9 zero crossings (forward/shuffle); high-cancellation0 range changes and1/13 zero crossings; max-fanin5/11/15 true range changes in forward/reverse/shuffle, plus1 reverse zero crossing. A one-pre-event-gain software screen remains exact on the two small-step sites, but max-fanin one-gain-per-event ends−8.11mV forward, +6.41mV reverse and−33.47mV shuffled, with maximum prefix error310mV. The max-fanin result requires the range selector to change gain *during* the pulse as the state crosses a boundary, not once per event. Preserve this as demand sizing; next physically characterize a fixed state capacitor, live analog range selector and weighted pulse-duration source through those crossing events.

### Current immediate simulation blocker — SKY130 MIM model population
The combined32-bit selector/weighted MIM top is physically routed and passes Magic DRC0 and unique Netgen LVS, but the standard PDK transient still fails the5ms state-retention gate (≈10.3mV common-mode rise). The isolated MIM model sweep shows near-zero state-clamp current with1/16 devices,≈18–20pA with256/512, then a rise to≈157pA as the ordered netlist grows through640–768 devices, even with every plate/state clamped to equal VCM. This points to a cumulative/model-population interaction; the exact parameter or simulator mechanism is unresolved. Proceed with prefix-to-bank/model correlation and controlled model runs. The ideal-zero-via-resistance diagnostic timed out and is not a viable workaround. Resume readout/full-float integration only after a standard-model 5ms trim-array run is stable. Evidence is logged in `docs/RESULTS.md` and the `sky130_trim_array_state_drift_diag_n*.json` artifacts.

Model correlation narrowed the mismatch further: each isolated 255-cell coarse-P/fine-P/fine-N bank has about18pA clamp current, but populated-bank interactions raise it to≈157pA/rail. Common-plate wiring does not cure the issue; common-state wiring reduces it to≈122pA. The low-ohm shortcut is not usable (transient stiffness). Next use paired-bank/subcircuit scaling and a second simulator or minimal reproducer; retain the standard PDK model until causality is established.

Independent simulator evidence: official ngspice47 with KLU gives the same full-array clamp current as ngspice36 (≈156.7pA/state). Its integrated 5ms run did not finish in the bounded180s and stopped at≈0.78ms; partial result rejected. This strengthens the conclusion that the symptom is in model/network setup, not simply an old ngspice build. Maintain the previous ngspice36 completed 5ms result as the acceptance reference while diagnosing the PDK model connection/current paths.

The apparent DC current is a solver/model operating-point sensitivity: it exists before transient in both ngspice36 and ngspice47 (≈156.7pA/state at identical 0.9V terminals), survives a 1e15Ω diagnostic cap bleed, and changes by orders of magnitude when the diagnostic via-resistance value is swept. Do not interpret the pA result as device leakage or alter production PDK parameters. Continue with model branch-current inspection/minimal reproduction and a numerically defensible initialization path; standard-model5ms state stability remains open.

Update to the current MIM transient gate: standard PDK series-R model drift is not solved; however its internal ESR time constant is≈0.93fs and explicit RC expansion has the same startup artifact. A reduced-order integrated PEX transient preserving 9.52fF units, routed parasitics and selector devices passes a 4.9ms hold window at TT/27°C (≤0.174µV post-update motion). Use this only for long-timescale module tests; retain the original PDK result as an unresolved device-level simulator limitation. Next sweep trim-code patterns and PVT, then integrate readout and original-float replay. Full analog accuracy and full-chip assembly/layout are still incomplete.

Updated module progress: routed array retention now passes in the reduced-order transient at TT/27, SS/0, FF/85, and the routed array plus extracted sample MIM/readout/latch gives deterministic SS/27 latch-sign passes at±0.25 and±0.537µV over5ms. The analog-value readout still has large asymmetric offset/gain errors and the+0.25 raw chopper sign is wrong. Keep analog/full-float objective active. Immediate next work: repair value transfer, calibrate threshold codes across PVT, then noise/mismatch and full-float event replay; full-chip assembly follows only after these gates.

2026-10-06 held-node leakage isolation: In the FF/85C +750mV, 5ms schematic 5V-TG screen, parking each overdriven off-switch source at its own initially commanded state instead of the fixed 0.9V shared bus leaves the 1V-overdriven TG low/high drift essentially unchanged: +141.6126/-75.4050mV on VCM and +141.6117/-75.4050mV parked. The NMOS-only and PMOS-only overdriven variants are likewise unchanged to the reported precision. By contrast, ordinary 0/1.8V off gates change the low-rail drift from +222.99mV to +12.71mV when parked. This separates a channel-voltage-sensitive component removed by off-gate overdrive from a remaining held-node leakage path; junction/body or model parasitics are plausible, but not proven without branch-current accounting. A fixed shared bus is the realistic comparison; parked per-state buses are diagnostic only. Do not pursue more simple gate overdrive. Next isolate transistor terminal currents and seek a storage node without a continuously reverse-biased diffusion before layout promotion. Source-hashed report: artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_shared_range_read_boundary.json.
2026-10-06 HV terminal-current diagnosis: Zero-volt probes were inserted at the body, off-bus and stored-node terminals of the schematic 5V TG without changing its FF/85C +750mV 5ms drift at reported precision. At 5ms the overdriven low-rail NMOS has body/source/state probe magnitudes 1.212/0.910/0.309pA, and the PMOS 2.426/0.910/1.512pA. On the high rail they are NMOS 2.494/0.910/1.581pA and PMOS 1.147/0.910/0.240pA. The signed body current agrees with the source-plus-state terminal currents within about 0.006pA for these devices. This directly locates the surviving pA path at the modeled body terminals, consistent with reverse-biased junction leakage; the gate terminal and transient capacitance are not independently probed. At the low stored node the PMOS state current overwhelms the opposing NMOS current; at the high node the NMOS dominates, explaining the 5ms compression. A viable compact store needs to remove or greatly reduce those continuously connected diffusion/body paths, not just add off-gate voltage. These are schematic 5V devices and diagnostic extracted MIM/MOS gate-cap, not a qualified physical HV cell. Source-hashed terminal values: artifacts/analog_asic/results/sky130_shared_range_read_boundary.json.

2026-10-06 off-gate diagnosis completed: FF refined body-tied TG overdrive-0.3/2.1V does not rescue hold. The both-well2mV state ends0.063858mV (normal gates0.062160mV); at300mV rail offset its565.066mV initially measured span ends5.277mV. Both simulator and existing initialization guard complete, but high-range initialization already loses34.9mV differential by2us. The earlier inference that the hot loss is channel leakage is not established. Body-to-state tying makes the off shared-bus source/body junction forward-biased on one device whenever state differs from VCM; gate overdrive cannot block that junction. Treat this as the next terminal-current hypothesis to verify rather than attributing the collapse to GMIN or selecting this body-tied access cell. Next probe source/body/state currents under low GMIN and compare fixed-body reverse-biased access with physical MOS-gate-cap storage, matched initialization and actual fixed shared bus. All launched simulations are terminal; full-float analog goal remains active, no qualified state macro or chip layout.


2026-10-06 source/body diagnosis: ngspice47 FF/85C low-GMIN DC probes using exact extracted body-tied TG transistor geometries complete.
